The paper's approach could contribute to more efficient and sustainable use of diffusion models, especially in workflows involving iterative image exploration and variation. The reported savings in compute, along with potential image quality improvements, are compelling.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Training-Free:** Avoids the need for retraining diffusion models, making it easily adaptable to existing systems.
    * **Scalable:** The hierarchical tree structure allows the method to scale favorably with the number of prompts.
    * **Improved or Comparable Quality:** Achieves comparable or better image quality compared to standard diffusion while using significantly less compute. This is particularly notable as it implies that the shared early diffusion may be advantageous in some cases, possibly by averaging out noise and providing a more stable foundation for later steps.
    * **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The paper includes various ablation studies that carefully assess the impact of different design choices, such as the clustering method and the condition modality of the diffusion model.
    * **Addresses a Practical Problem:** Text-to-image generation is often used to explore diverse image variations, emphasizing the value of compute reduction across multiple prompts.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on Gradual Detail Emergence:** The method's effectiveness relies on the diffusion model exhibiting a gradual emergence of fine details. Models that rapidly generate fine details may not benefit as much.
    * **Limited Diversity in Specific Scenarios:** As demonstrated in the paper, in certain highly structured data generations, the level of diversity may suffer, albeit the quality and lower compute is still maintained.
    * **CLIP Similarity as the Diversity Metric:** Using CLIP similarity as the evaluation metric might not reflect the true diversity, and may be biased to the models CLIP was trained on.
    * **Incremental Improvements:** While showing improvements in compute cost, the gains in VQA scores are marginal and can be interpreted as not extremely significant.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in efficient text-to-image generation, particularly in multi-prompt scenarios. It could also inspire further investigations into the generation dynamics of different diffusion models and the development of techniques for adapting diffusion step allocation for increased efficiency.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and practical approach to reducing the computational cost of multi-prompt text-to-image generation. The training-free nature, scalability, and potential for improved image quality are significant strengths. While the method has some limitations related to the detail emergence of the model and the scenarios may limit certain aspects of diversity, the paper provides a thorough evaluation and demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed technique across a range of datasets and models. The potential for real-world impact and the insights gained into diffusion model behavior justify a score reflecting a strong, but not transformative, contribution to the field.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MMG-Vid: Maximizing Marginal Gains at Segment-level and Token-level for Efficient Video LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21044v1)
- **Authors**: Junpeng Ma, Qizhe Zhang, Ming Lu, Zhibin Wang, Qiang Zhou, Jun Song, Shanghang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Video Large Language Models (VLLMs) excel in video understanding, but their excessive visual tokens pose a significant computational challenge for real-world applications. Current methods aim to enhance inference efficiency by visual token pruning. However, they do not consider the dynamic characteristics and temporal dependencies of video frames, as they perceive video understanding as a multi-frame task. To address these challenges, we propose MMG-Vid, a novel training-free visual token pruning framework that removes redundancy by Maximizing Marginal Gains at both segment-level and token-level. Specifically, we first divide the video into segments based on frame similarity, and then dynamically allocate the token budget for each segment to maximize the marginal gain of each segment. Subsequently, we propose a temporal-guided DPC algorithm that jointly models inter-frame uniqueness and intra-frame diversity, thereby maximizing the marginal gain of each token. By combining both stages, MMG-Vid can maximize the utilization of the limited token budget, significantly improving efficiency while maintaining strong performance. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MMG-Vid can maintain over 99.5% of the original performance, while effectively reducing 75% visual tokens and accelerating the prefilling stage by 3.9x on LLaVA-OneVision-7B. Code will be released soon.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMG-Vid, a training-free visual token pruning framework designed to improve the efficiency of Video Large Language Models (VLLMs).  The core idea is to maximize marginal gains at both the segment and token levels.  The method first divides the video into semantically coherent segments based on frame similarity.  Then, it dynamically allocates a token budget to each segment based on an iterative process that evaluates the marginal gain of each segment. Finally, it proposes a temporal-guided Density Peak Clustering (DPC) algorithm to jointly model inter-frame uniqueness and intra-frame diversity, aiming to maximize the marginal gain of each token.  Experiments on LLaVA-Video and LLaVA-OneVision demonstrate that MMG-Vid can significantly reduce the number of visual tokens and accelerate inference while maintaining strong performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel ideas:
    * **Marginal Gain Formulation:** Reframing token pruning as a problem of maximizing marginal gain at both segment and token levels is a valuable conceptual shift. Prior work often treated importance and diversity as separate, disjoint processes, whereas MMG-Vid aims for a unified approach.
    * **Dynamic Segment Budgeting:**  Dynamically allocating token budgets based on segment content is a good idea.  While the concept of importance-aware token allocation isn't entirely new, the iterative, marginal-gain-based approach is a significant improvement over static allocation strategies.  The way they allocate the remaining extra-budget (Rextra) is reasonably novel.
    * **Temporal-Guided DPC (TG-DPC):** Incorporating temporal information into the DPC algorithm is a solid contribution to the token pruning method, addressing the static, frame-by-frame processing limitations of many existing methods.  Modeling inter-frame uniqueness and intra-frame diversity together allows better retention of important content.

* **Significance:** The work has the potential to be significant. The primary motivation - improving the efficiency of VLLMs - is crucial for their real-world deployment. The reported results (significant token reduction with minimal performance loss and increased prefilling speed) are compelling. If the method can be readily applied to other VLLMs and datasets, it can facilitate broader adoption of VLLMs in resource-constrained environments.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Training-Free:** A significant strength is the training-free nature of the method. This avoids the computational cost and data requirements of training-based token compression, making it more readily applicable to existing VLLMs.
    * **Principled Approach:** The marginal gain formulation provides a solid theoretical foundation for the token pruning process.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate that MMG-Vid consistently outperforms existing methods across multiple datasets and VLLMs.  The ablation studies are useful for understanding the contribution of each component.
    * **Clear Writing and Organization:** The paper is generally well-written and structured, making it relatively easy to understand the method and its motivation.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Complexity Analysis:** Although the method is training-free, the paper lacks a detailed analysis of the computational complexity of the proposed algorithms. Evaluating the overall computational savings, considering the overhead of segmenting the video and computing similarities, would be beneficial.
    * **Sensitivity to Hyperparameters:** The paper mentions setting the similarity threshold (τ) and the balancing parameter (λ). A more detailed discussion of the sensitivity of the performance to these hyperparameters, and guidelines on how to set them for different datasets and VLLMs, would improve the practicality of the method.
    * **Limited Scope of VLLMs Evaluated:** While experiments cover two VLLMs (LLaVA-OneVision and LLaVA-Video), evaluating a broader range of architectures would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    * **DPC Parameter Selection:** The DPC Algorithm has its own parameter sensitivities regarding the local density. The paper mentions the DPC-KNN approach but doesn't explicitly state what value of K is used or the importance of it.

* **Potential Influence:** The work has the potential to influence the development of more efficient VLLMs.  The marginal gain formulation and dynamic segment budgeting could be adopted or extended by other researchers.  The TG-DPC algorithm could inspire further exploration of temporal modeling in token compression techniques. The strong empirical results are likely to attract interest from both academic and industrial researchers working on VLLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to visual token pruning in VLLMs.  While there are areas for improvement, such as a more detailed computational complexity analysis and hyperparameter sensitivity analysis, the core ideas and empirical results demonstrate a significant advance over existing methods.  The training-free nature and strong performance are likely to make MMG-Vid a valuable tool for improving the efficiency of VLLMs. The combination of a solid theoretical approach (marginal gain maximization) with practical results makes it a substantial contribution. The weaknesses detract from the overall score, but the paper presents enough compelling strengths that the positive aspects outweigh those limitations.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Veritas: Generalizable Deepfake Detection via Pattern-Aware Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21048v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Tan, Jun Lan, Zichang Tan, Ajian Liu, Chuanbiao Song, Senyuan Shi, Huijia Zhu, Weiqiang Wang, Jun Wan, Zhen Lei
- **Abstract**: Deepfake detection remains a formidable challenge due to the complex and evolving nature of fake content in real-world scenarios. However, existing academic benchmarks suffer from severe discrepancies from industrial practice, typically featuring homogeneous training sources and low-quality testing images, which hinder the practical deployments of current detectors. To mitigate this gap, we introduce HydraFake, a dataset that simulates real-world challenges with hierarchical generalization testing. Specifically, HydraFake involves diversified deepfake techniques and in-the-wild forgeries, along with rigorous training and evaluation protocol, covering unseen model architectures, emerging forgery techniques and novel data domains. Building on this resource, we propose Veritas, a multi-modal large language model (MLLM) based deepfake detector. Different from vanilla chain-of-thought (CoT), we introduce pattern-aware reasoning that involves critical reasoning patterns such as "planning" and "self-reflection" to emulate human forensic process. We further propose a two-stage training pipeline to seamlessly internalize such deepfake reasoning capacities into current MLLMs. Experiments on HydraFake dataset reveal that although previous detectors show great generalization on cross-model scenarios, they fall short on unseen forgeries and data domains. Our Veritas achieves significant gains across different OOD scenarios, and is capable of delivering transparent and faithful detection outputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and rigorous evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "VERITAS: GENERALIZABLE DEEPFAKE DETECTION VIA PATTERN-AWARE REASONING" addresses the challenge of deepfake detection, which is hindered by the gap between academic benchmarks and real-world scenarios. To bridge this gap, the authors introduce HydraFake, a new dataset designed to simulate real-world deepfake challenges with hierarchical generalization testing. They also propose VERITAS, a multi-modal large language model (MLLM) based deepfake detector incorporating pattern-aware reasoning (including planning and self-reflection) to mimic human forensic processes. A two-stage training pipeline is used to internalize deepfake reasoning capacities into MLLMs. Experiments on HydraFake show VERITAS achieves significantly better generalization, particularly across unseen forgeries and data domains, compared to existing detectors. The model also provides transparent and faithful detection outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates significant novelty in several aspects:

    *   The **HydraFake dataset** is a valuable contribution, addressing a critical deficiency in existing deepfake datasets. It aims to better simulate real-world scenarios with diversified deepfake techniques, in-the-wild forgeries, and hierarchical generalization testing. This focus on realistic challenges is an improvement over commonly used homogeneous datasets.
    *   The **VERITAS model**, which uses pattern-aware reasoning, is a novel approach to deepfake detection. By incorporating human-like reasoning patterns (planning, self-reflection) into the MLLM, the authors aim to improve the model's ability to generalize to unseen data. The two-stage training pipeline is also a notable methodological contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by:

    *   Identifying and addressing the limitations of existing deepfake detection benchmarks.
    *   Providing a more realistic dataset (HydraFake) that can be used to evaluate and improve deepfake detectors.
    *   Developing a novel MLLM-based detector (VERITAS) that demonstrates improved generalization performance compared to existing methods.
    *   Emphasizing the importance of reasoning and explainability in deepfake detection.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly presents the problem, proposed solution, and experimental results.
    *   The HydraFake dataset is a valuable resource for the research community, addressing a critical gap in existing benchmarks.
    *   The VERITAS model achieves state-of-the-art performance on the HydraFake dataset, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed pattern-aware reasoning approach.
    *   The paper provides a thorough ablation study and analysis of the different components of the VERITAS model.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation, more qualitative examples of the model's reasoning process on challenging examples could further highlight its advantages.
    *   Although promising, MLLMs are computationally intensive. The paper would be strengthened by addressing the computational efficiency of VERITAS, discussing its resource requirements for training and deployment, and possibly exploring avenues for optimization.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of deepfake detection by:

    *   Motivating the development of more realistic and challenging datasets.
    *   Inspiring new approaches to deepfake detection that incorporate reasoning and explainability.
    *   Facilitating the development of more robust and generalizable deepfake detectors that can be deployed in real-world scenarios.

    In summary, the paper is valuable, has novelty in its dataset construction and approach to reasoning and generalization, and it tackles a relevant and growing problem. However, there are a couple areas where the experimental results or discussion could be improved.

**Score: 8.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of deepfake detection, addressing a critical gap between academic benchmarks and real-world scenarios. The HydraFake dataset is a valuable resource, and the VERITAS model demonstrates improved generalization performance compared to existing methods. While further improvements, especially on scalability and broader qualitative examples, would strengthen the work, it represents a significant step forward and is therefore rated highly. The paper merits the attention of the deepfake detection community.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Enabling Equitable Access to Trustworthy Financial Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21051v1)
- **Authors**: William Jurayj, Nils Holzenberger, Benjamin Van Durme
- **Abstract**: According to the United States Internal Revenue Service, ''the average American spends $\$270$ and 13 hours filing their taxes''. Even beyond the U.S., tax filing requires complex reasoning, combining application of overlapping rules with numerical calculations. Because errors can incur costly penalties, any automated system must deliver high accuracy and auditability, making modern large language models (LLMs) poorly suited for this task. We propose an approach that integrates LLMs with a symbolic solver to calculate tax obligations. We evaluate variants of this system on the challenging StAtutory Reasoning Assessment (SARA) dataset, and include a novel method for estimating the cost of deploying such a system based on real-world penalties for tax errors. We further show how combining up-front translation of plain-text rules into formal logic programs, combined with intelligently retrieved exemplars for formal case representations, can dramatically improve performance on this task and reduce costs to well below real-world averages. Our results demonstrate the promise and economic feasibility of neuro-symbolic architectures for increasing equitable access to reliable tax assistance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of providing equitable and trustworthy access to financial reasoning, specifically tax preparation. Recognizing the limitations of large language models (LLMs) in this domain due to accuracy and auditability concerns, the authors propose a neuro-symbolic approach that integrates LLMs with a symbolic solver. The LLM is used to translate statutory text and taxpayer information into formal logic programs, which are then processed by a trusted execution engine (Prolog). They evaluate their method on the StAtutory Reasoning Assessment (SARA) dataset, demonstrating that their approach significantly improves performance compared to direct LLM application. Further, they introduce a novel method for estimating the cost of deploying such a system, considering real-world penalties for tax errors, and show how intelligently combining plain-text rules with formal case exemplars can reduce these costs below real-world averages. The results highlight the promise and feasibility of neuro-symbolic architectures for increasing equitable access to reliable tax assistance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a few key novel ideas. Firstly, the specific integration of LLMs with a symbolic solver (Prolog) to solve statutory reasoning is a valuable contribution. While neuro-symbolic approaches are not entirely new, the application to the tax domain with a focus on accuracy, auditability, and cost analysis is novel. Secondly, the approach of translating statutory text and taxpayer information into formal logic and then querying with a symbolic solver, while not new to AI generally, hadn’t been explicitly explored with LLMs for this type of task to this extent and this dataset. The inclusion of a novel cost model based on real-world tax penalties is a practical addition and helps to contextualize the performance improvements. Finally, the concept of leveraging few-shot learning with intelligent retrieval of "precedential" cases within the symbolic reasoning framework seems to be a unique aspect.

*   **Significance:** The work tackles a real-world problem with significant societal impact. Tax preparation can be complex, time-consuming, and costly, particularly for lower-income individuals. By demonstrating the potential of an AI-assisted system to improve accuracy and reduce costs, the paper opens doors to democratizing access to reliable financial advice. The focus on auditability is crucial, as it addresses concerns about transparency and trustworthiness, essential for widespread adoption. The experiments conducted are rigorous, leveraging a challenging benchmark dataset and comparing performance against strong baseline LLMs. The error cost analysis provides an economic argument for adopting this approach.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined neuro-symbolic architecture.
    *   Rigorous experimental evaluation on a challenging dataset.
    *   Novel cost model for evaluating real-world implications.
    *   Detailed analysis of model performance and error patterns.
    *   Good quality writing and clear presentation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Reliance on gold-standard statutory translations: While the paper explores scenarios with and without them, the best performance is obtained by manual translations. The paper should thoroughly consider how the cost of creating and maintaining these translations impacts overall real-world deployment. While the paper points to some companies and projects that do so, the scale of those efforts is not fully addressed. The generalizability of the developed system to other tax laws or other legal domains may depend on the ease of creating analogous gold-standard rules.
    *   Limited discussion of scalability. The paper doesn't discuss the performance with larger knowledge bases.
    *   Limited evaluation across various demographic groups. Given the focus on equitable access, evaluation should emphasize performance across different income levels or other relevant demographic categories. This aspect is not properly explored.

*   **Overall Impact:** The paper contributes valuable insights into the potential of neuro-symbolic systems for financial reasoning. The results and analysis can inform future research on developing more accurate, trustworthy, and cost-effective AI systems for tax assistance and other high-stakes domains.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by demonstrating a practical and effective approach for combining LLMs with symbolic reasoning to address a real-world problem. The novelty lies in the specific application of this architecture to tax preparation, the integration of a cost model based on real-world penalties, and the innovative use of intelligent exemplar retrieval. However, the reliance on gold-standard statutory translations, limited discussion of scalability, and lack of demographic-specific evaluation are notable limitations. Overall, the paper is a strong contribution with the potential to influence future research and development in AI-assisted financial services, but the points mentioned above hold it back from receiving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mixture of Contexts for Long Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21058v1)
- **Authors**: Shengqu Cai, Ceyuan Yang, Lvmin Zhang, Yuwei Guo, Junfei Xiao, Ziyan Yang, Yinghao Xu, Zhenheng Yang, Alan Yuille, Leonidas Guibas, Maneesh Agrawala, Lu Jiang, Gordon Wetzstein
- **Abstract**: Long video generation is fundamentally a long context memory problem: models must retain and retrieve salient events across a long range without collapsing or drifting. However, scaling diffusion transformers to generate long-context videos is fundamentally limited by the quadratic cost of self-attention, which makes memory and computation intractable and difficult to optimize for long sequences. We recast long-context video generation as an internal information retrieval task and propose a simple, learnable sparse attention routing module, Mixture of Contexts (MoC), as an effective long-term memory retrieval engine. In MoC, each query dynamically selects a few informative chunks plus mandatory anchors (caption, local windows) to attend to, with causal routing that prevents loop closures. As we scale the data and gradually sparsify the routing, the model allocates compute to salient history, preserving identities, actions, and scenes over minutes of content. Efficiency follows as a byproduct of retrieval (near-linear scaling), which enables practical training and synthesis, and the emergence of memory and consistency at the scale of minutes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Mixture of Contexts for Long Video Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of long-context video generation by proposing a novel attention mechanism called Mixture of Contexts (MoC). MoC tackles the quadratic complexity of self-attention in diffusion transformers (DiTs) by learning a sparse context routing policy.  Instead of attending to all tokens, each query dynamically selects a few relevant chunks of video content (frames, shots, captions) using a trainable, parameter-free top-k router, along with mandatory anchor links (captions, local windows). A causal mask prevents loop closures. This sparse attention mechanism significantly reduces computational costs while preserving long-term coherence and consistency in generated videos. Experiments show that MoC achieves near-linear scaling, enabling the generation of minute-long videos at speeds comparable to shorter sequences while maintaining and sometimes exceeding the fidelity of dense attention baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty lies in the learned sparse context routing policy embodied by MoC. Existing approaches for efficient video generation rely on fixed sparsity patterns, compression techniques, or hard-coded selection rules. MoC's key contribution is the dynamic and adaptive nature of its chunk selection, enabling the model to learn which context is most relevant for each query token. The use of mean-pooling keys for efficient chunk representation, combined with the top-k routing, is clever and contributes to both efficiency and expressiveness.  The integration of mandatory anchors (cross-modal and intra-shot links) to stabilize training and ensure baseline fidelity is also a good design choice. Causality masking is standard but necessary.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of video generation. The ability to generate long, coherent videos is a crucial step toward more realistic and useful applications. MoC addresses a key bottleneck—the computational cost of long-range attention—and provides a practical solution for scaling diffusion models to longer sequences. Demonstrating minute-level video generation with improved speed and competitive quality is a valuable contribution. The emphasis on data-driven context selection, rather than relying on hand-engineered heuristics, could pave the way for more robust and generalizable video generation systems. The simplicity of MoC (mean-pooling, top-k, with anchors and causal routing) enhances its potential adoption and further research. The implementation details described, focusing on var-len flash-attention, are also valuable. The zero-shot experiment where MOA is plugged into a trained network further underlines its potential.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a critical problem:** Long-context video generation is a significant challenge.
*   **Novel approach:** MoC's learned sparse attention routing is a promising technique.
*   **Improved efficiency:** Near-linear scaling enables longer video generation.
*   **Competitive performance:** Achieves comparable or superior quality to dense baselines.
*   **Comprehensive experiments:** Evaluated on both single-shot and multi-shot video generation.
*   **Clear implementation details:** The paper is clearly written and provides sufficient information for implementation.
* **Plug-and-play applicability:** MoC can be plugged in trained video generation networks.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited architectural scope**: While the modularity of MoC is appealing, the performance improvements were largely seen within a MMDiT architecture. Additional explorations on different architectures would provide more insight.
*   **Dataset limited**: Limited to a relatively specialized dataset. Evaluation on video world model datasets would add significant strength.
*   **Simplistic router**: The mean-pooling + dot product router might be a bottleneck, with the performance highly dependent on data.  Exploring other router implementations (e.g., with trainable parameters) may further improve performance. The reliance on data can be seen as a trade-off for simplicity and training stability.
*   **Ablations limited:** While the ablation studies are sufficient, more detailed analysis of the impact of various parameters and design choices would be beneficial.
*   **Perception scores**: The perception scores (VBench) could be more clearly analyzed in terms of the specific trade-offs in performance due to the sparse attention.

**Potential influence:**

The paper is well-positioned to influence the field. It provides a practical and effective solution to a critical problem, and the simplicity of the MoC approach makes it accessible to other researchers. It could inspire further work on learned sparse attention mechanisms for video generation and other long-context tasks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates significant novelty in tackling long-context video generation and presents a practical, well-implemented solution. The achieved improvements in efficiency without sacrificing quality are noteworthy. However, the scope remains limited, and some aspects of the design could be explored further. The weaknesses prevent it from reaching the very high scores. Despite these shortcomings, the overall contribution is substantial, making it a strong and influential paper.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### OnGoal: Tracking and Visualizing Conversational Goals in Multi-Turn Dialogue with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21061v1)
- **Authors**: Adam Coscia, Shunan Guo, Eunyee Koh, Alex Endert
- **Abstract**: As multi-turn dialogues with large language models (LLMs) grow longer and more complex, how can users better evaluate and review progress on their conversational goals? We present OnGoal, an LLM chat interface that helps users better manage goal progress. OnGoal provides real-time feedback on goal alignment through LLM-assisted evaluation, explanations for evaluation results with examples, and overviews of goal progression over time, enabling users to navigate complex dialogues more effectively. Through a study with 20 participants on a writing task, we evaluate OnGoal against a baseline chat interface without goal tracking. Using OnGoal, participants spent less time and effort to achieve their goals while exploring new prompting strategies to overcome miscommunication, suggesting tracking and visualizing goals can enhance engagement and resilience in LLM dialogues. Our findings inspired design implications for future LLM chat interfaces that improve goal communication, reduce cognitive load, enhance interactivity, and enable feedback to improve LLM performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary, critical evaluation, and justified novelty score for the provided paper, focusing on the core contribution and limitations:

**Summary:**

The paper "OnGoal: Tracking and Visualizing Conversational Goals in Multi-Turn Dialogue with Large Language Models" introduces OnGoal, a chat interface augmenting Large Language Model (LLM) interactions with goal-tracking visualizations. OnGoal infers, merges, and evaluates user's conversational goals throughout the interaction, providing in-situ feedback on goal alignment via LLM-assisted evaluation, explanations, and examples.  A user study compared OnGoal against a baseline chat interface, showing participants spent less time and effort toward achieving goals. It also reveals that the tracking and visualization encourages the explorations of new prompting strategies. The paper concludes with design implications for future LLM chat interfaces, focusing on improved goal communication and interaction with LLMs in extended dialogues.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper addresses a relevant and increasingly important problem: the difficulties users face in managing conversational goals during multi-turn interactions with LLMs.  Existing chat interfaces can become overwhelming due to the sheer length of the conversation and the difficulty in tracking if previous goals are being addressed.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Problem & Solution:** The paper tackles a concrete problem that arises with more complex LLM interactions. OnGoal provides a tangible interface solution with goal tracking that can enhance engagement and resilience in LLM dialogues
    *   **LLM-Assisted Goal Pipeline:** Integrating an LLM to infer, merge, and evaluate goals is a good design choice. This automation reduces the user's cognitive load by automatically tracking and organizing goals.
    *   **In-Situ Visualizations & Feedback:** Visualizing goal progress and providing explanations inline with the chat is a smart design choice for the UX; the real-time feedback on goal alignment helps navigate complex dialogues more efficiently.
    *   **User Study & Insights:** The user study provides empirical evidence suggesting OnGoal enhances user engagement and the ability to navigate LLM dialogues, highlighting the real-time effects on the writing task.
    *   **Design Implications:** The study findings help to guide future LLM chat interfaces.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The study focuses on a writing task, limiting the generalizability of the findings to other LLM use cases (e.g., information retrieval, code generation). While writing is a relevant task, more diverse tasks would increase the impact of the paper.
    *   **Pipeline Performance Evaluation:** A formal evaluation of the accuracy of the "infer," "merge," and "evaluate" steps in the pipeline is missing. While user evaluations are useful, a benchmark quantifying the actual performance is needed.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While practical and well-executed, the core ideas of visualizing goals, providing feedback, and using LLMs to assist with management are not entirely new. It primarily focuses on the integration of these methods into a specific context (LLM chat).
    *   **Lack of comparative Analysis with DST:** Existing research on dialogue state tracking (DST) in natural language processing (NLP) has established methods for modeling user goals and tracking conversation states. The paper does not adequately compare or contrast the methodology to DST.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its practical application and validation of a goal-tracking interface for LLM interactions. It highlights the importance of providing users with tools to manage and review their goals during complex conversations, thus improving user experience and outcomes. It provides concrete design recommendations that can inform the development of more effective LLM interfaces.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper addresses a relevant problem and provides a usable solution backed by empirical evidence, it suffers from limitations in generalizability and novelty. It utilizes well-established techniques and integrates them into a specific context. The user study is valuable, but the lack of formal evaluation of the pipeline and comparison to DST undermines the overall contribution. The idea of tracking the goal in multi-turn conversations is significant.

**Score: 6**

The score reflects a paper that is a solid, incremental contribution with practical value and empirical support, but lacking in theoretical novelty or broad applicability. The limitations in generalizability also impact the overall impact on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### OneReward: Unified Mask-Guided Image Generation via Multi-Task Human Preference Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21066v1)
- **Authors**: Yuan Gong, Xionghui Wang, Jie Wu, Shiyin Wang, Yitong Wang, Xinglong Wu
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we introduce OneReward, a unified reinforcement learning framework that enhances the model's generative capabilities across multiple tasks under different evaluation criteria using only \textit{One Reward} model. By employing a single vision-language model (VLM) as the generative reward model, which can distinguish the winner and loser for a given task and a given evaluation criterion, it can be effectively applied to multi-task generation models, particularly in contexts with varied data and diverse task objectives. We utilize OneReward for mask-guided image generation, which can be further divided into several sub-tasks such as image fill, image extend, object removal, and text rendering, involving a binary mask as the edit area. Although these domain-specific tasks share same conditioning paradigm, they differ significantly in underlying data distributions and evaluation metrics. Existing methods often rely on task-specific supervised fine-tuning (SFT), which limits generalization and training efficiency. Building on OneReward, we develop Seedream 3.0 Fill, a mask-guided generation model trained via multi-task reinforcement learning directly on a pre-trained base model, eliminating the need for task-specific SFT. Experimental results demonstrate that our unified edit model consistently outperforms both commercial and open-source competitors, such as Ideogram, Adobe Photoshop, and FLUX Fill [Pro], across multiple evaluation dimensions. Code and model are available at: https://one-reward.github.io
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "OneReward," a novel framework that aims to unify mask-guided image generation across multiple tasks (image inpainting, outpainting, object removal, text rendering) using a single reinforcement learning (RL) setup and a single visual-language model (VLM) as the reward function. The core idea is to train a model, "Seedream 3.0 Fill," by directly applying multi-task RL to a pre-trained base model, eliminating the need for task-specific supervised fine-tuning (SFT). The authors claim their unified model outperforms both commercial and open-source alternatives across various evaluation criteria. They formulate the reward function to enable a single VLM to distinguish between tasks and evaluation criteria.  They also propose a "dynamic" reinforcement learning strategy to address stability and reward hacking concerns, where the EMA model is directly reused as the reference model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the *integration* of several existing techniques in a unified and task-agnostic manner. Using RLHF for image generation is not new, nor is the use of VLMs as reward functions. However, the specific combination of multi-task RL, a single VLM reward model that is conditioned on task category and evaluation metric, and a dynamic reinforcement learning strategy (using the EMA model as the reference) to avoid reward hacking, represents a significant contribution. It simplifies training and enhances generalization compared to task-specific SFT or having separate reward models for each task. The use of the *same* VLM to act as reward function of various generation is a novelty.
*   **Significance:** The potential significance of the paper is high. The ability to train a single model to perform well on a diverse set of image editing tasks is valuable. The reported improvements over existing commercial and open-source systems, if validated, could have a substantial impact on the field. The reduction in training complexity (avoiding task-specific SFT) is also a significant advantage. The open-sourcing of "FLUX Fill [dev][OneReward]" contributes to the community and enables further research. By dynamically adjust reference and policy model, RL training becomes more stable.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Unified Framework:** The OneReward framework offers a practical way to consolidate multiple image editing tasks into a single model, simplifying training and deployment.
    *   **Performance:** Experimental results suggest state-of-the-art performance across different tasks and evaluation metrics.
    *   **Efficiency:** Eliminating the need for task-specific SFT saves computational resources and improves generalization.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:** Releasing the modified FLUX Fill model promotes reproducibility and future research.
    *   **Dynamic Training:** The dynamic reference model addresses a crucial stability problem in RLHF, preventing reward hacking and enabling consistent training.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on VLM Quality:** The entire framework relies heavily on the quality and generalization ability of the underlying VLM. The VLM might have inherent biases that affect the outcome.
    *   **Complex RL Training:** RL training can be notoriously unstable and require careful tuning of hyperparameters. While the paper mentions a "dynamic" RL strategy, details of hyperparameter selection and robustness analysis are somewhat limited. More extensive ablation studies and sensitivity analysis are needed.
    *   **Human Preference Dataset:** the high quality dataset for human preferece annotation is crucial for training a powerful multi-tasked generation model. However, details of building such dataset can be further elaborated.
    *   **Limited Task Coverage:** While the paper covers four important tasks, there are other relevant image editing tasks (e.g., style transfer, colorization) that could be included to further demonstrate the framework's versatility.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It could serve as a baseline for unified image editing frameworks based on RLHF.
    *   The OneReward framework could be extended to other generative tasks beyond image editing.
    *   The dynamic RL strategy could be adopted in other RLHF applications to improve stability.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel *integration* of existing techniques into a unified framework for multi-task image editing, demonstrating strong empirical results and providing a practical solution to a challenging problem. The dynamic training methodology also appears robust. However, the work is heavily reliant on the underlying VLM, and the details regarding RL hyperparameter tuning and dataset selection could be further elaborated. While the unified reward training approach is novel, it would be benificial to include experiments by utilizing different reward training strategies such as ReFL or DPO. Overall, the paper makes a significant contribution to the field but would benefit from more detailed robustness and ablation studies.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### First-Place Solution to NeurIPS 2024 Invisible Watermark Removal Challenge
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21072v1)
- **Authors**: Fahad Shamshad, Tameem Bakr, Yahia Shaaban, Noor Hussein, Karthik Nandakumar, Nils Lukas
- **Abstract**: Content watermarking is an important tool for the authentication and copyright protection of digital media. However, it is unclear whether existing watermarks are robust against adversarial attacks. We present the winning solution to the NeurIPS 2024 Erasing the Invisible challenge, which stress-tests watermark robustness under varying degrees of adversary knowledge. The challenge consisted of two tracks: a black-box and beige-box track, depending on whether the adversary knows which watermarking method was used by the provider. For the beige-box track, we leverage an adaptive VAE-based evasion attack, with a test-time optimization and color-contrast restoration in CIELAB space to preserve the image's quality. For the black-box track, we first cluster images based on their artifacts in the spatial or frequency-domain. Then, we apply image-to-image diffusion models with controlled noise injection and semantic priors from ChatGPT-generated captions to each cluster with optimized parameter settings. Empirical evaluations demonstrate that our method successfully achieves near-perfect watermark removal (95.7%) with negligible impact on the residual image's quality. We hope that our attacks inspire the development of more robust image watermarking methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents the winning solution to the NeurIPS 2024 Erasing the Invisible challenge, focusing on removing invisible watermarks from images.  It tackles both beige-box (where the watermarking method is known) and black-box scenarios (no prior knowledge).  For beige-box attacks, the solution uses an adaptive VAE-based approach with test-time optimization and color/contrast restoration. For black-box attacks, it clusters images based on spatial and frequency domain artifacts, then applies image-to-image diffusion models guided by ChatGPT-generated captions with optimized parameters per cluster.  The results demonstrate near-perfect watermark removal with minimal impact on image quality.  The paper aims to inspire the development of more robust watermarking methods by exposing vulnerabilities in current techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    The paper's novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of techniques and their adaptation to the specific challenges posed by the NeurIPS competition.  While individual components like VAE-based image manipulation, diffusion models, and clustering techniques aren't entirely new, the paper's contribution is in orchestrating these tools in a targeted way to effectively remove watermarks under varying levels of attacker knowledge. In beige-box setting, the test-time optimization of the VAE and color-contrast restoration are a good additions to a fairly standard approach, enhancing perceptual quality. The black-box strategy, particularly the clustering based on observed artifacts and the subsequent application of caption-guided diffusion models with cluster-specific parameters, demonstrates a creative and resourceful approach. The use of ChatGPT-generated captions for semantic guidance in the diffusion process is also a valuable contribution. Showing spatial translations can also disrupt watermarks is useful.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper holds significant value in the context of digital media security and provenance. Exposing weaknesses in existing watermarking schemes is crucial for motivating the development of more robust defenses.  The paper's success in removing watermarks, even in the black-box setting, highlights the limitations of current watermarking technologies and the need for more sophisticated solutions. This is particularly important given the increasing prevalence of AI-generated content and the need to verify its authenticity. The work directly addresses the call for rigorous evaluation and realistic threat models in watermarking research. The successful attack strategies presented can inform the design of future watermarking methods that are more resistant to adversarial manipulation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-structured presentation of the methodology.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation with quantitative and qualitative results.
    *   Successful performance in a competitive benchmark, demonstrating practical effectiveness.
    *   Addresses a timely and relevant problem.
    *   Combination of multiple techniques to create a powerful solution.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The individual techniques are not groundbreaking, relying on known methods adapted for the task.
    *   While the approach is effective, it might be computationally expensive due to the use of diffusion models and per-image optimization.
    *   The paper's focus is primarily on *attack* strategies. While this is valuable for understanding vulnerabilities, further work could explore how these attacks can directly inform the design of stronger watermarking schemes.

**Justification for Score:**

I am giving this paper a score of 7. While the individual techniques are not entirely new, the paper effectively *integrates* and *adapts* these methods to achieve state-of-the-art watermark removal performance, especially in challenging black-box scenarios. The use of ChatGPT for semantic guidance in diffusion is insightful. The detailed methodology and comprehensive evaluation are valuable contributions to the field, demonstrating practical vulnerabilities and inspiring the design of more robust defenses. The paper’s main impact is inspiring stronger watermarking techniques. The solution could also be computationally expensive.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning to Generate Unit Test via Adversarial Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21107v1)
- **Authors**: Dongjun Lee, Changho Hwang, Kimin Lee
- **Abstract**: Unit testing is a core practice in programming, enabling systematic evaluation of programs produced by human developers or large language models (LLMs). Given the challenges in writing comprehensive unit tests, LLMs have been employed to automate test generation, yet methods for training LLMs to produce high-quality tests remain underexplored. In this work, we propose UTRL, a novel reinforcement learning framework that trains an LLM to generate high-quality unit tests given a programming instruction. Our key idea is to iteratively train two LLMs, the unit test generator and the code generator, in an adversarial manner via reinforcement learning. The unit test generator is trained to maximize a discrimination reward, which reflects its ability to produce tests that expose faults in the code generator's solutions, and the code generator is trained to maximize a code reward, which reflects its ability to produce solutions that pass the unit tests generated by the test generator. In our experiments, we demonstrate that unit tests generated by Qwen3-4B trained via UTRL show higher quality compared to unit tests generated by the same model trained via supervised fine-tuning on human-written ground-truth unit tests, yielding code evaluations that more closely align with those induced by the ground-truth tests. Moreover, Qwen3-4B trained with UTRL outperforms frontier models such as GPT-4.1 in generating high-quality unit tests, highlighting the effectiveness of UTRL in training LLMs for this task.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UTRL, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework for training large language models (LLMs) to generate high-quality unit tests. UTRL employs an adversarial training approach, iteratively training a unit test generator and a code generator. The unit test generator is trained to maximize a "discrimination reward," which reflects its ability to generate tests that expose faults in the code generator's solutions. Conversely, the code generator is trained to maximize a "code reward," indicating its ability to produce solutions that pass the generated unit tests. Experiments demonstrate that unit tests generated by a Qwen3-4B model trained using UTRL exhibit higher quality compared to those generated by the same model trained via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on human-written unit tests. Moreover, UTRL-trained Qwen3-4B outperforms models like GPT-4.1 in generating high-quality unit tests.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of adversarial training between a test generator and a code generator within an RL framework is a valuable contribution. Existing approaches primarily rely on supervised fine-tuning with human-annotated tests or simpler reward schemes. UTRL's approach of learning effective rewards through adversarial interaction distinguishes it. The novel definition of the "discrimination reward" also contributes to the originality of the work. The use of RL to automatically generate these rewards can be seen as beneficial.

*   **Significance:** High-quality unit tests are crucial for software development and the reliable operation of LLMs. Automating their generation effectively can significantly reduce development costs and improve code quality. If UTRL consistently outperforms SFT and other baselines, it has the potential to become a valuable tool in the field. The demonstrated performance gain over even GPT-4.1 highlights this potential significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides convincing evidence of UTRL's effectiveness through various experiments, including comparisons with SFT baselines, GPT-4.1, and an ablation study. The performance boost in "best-of-N sampling" and the improved "unit test fidelity" metrics are compelling.
    *   **Scalability:** By removing the need for explicit unit test labels and relying on only instruction-code pairs, UTRL presents a more scalable paradigm than SFT for training LLMs in unit test generation.
    *   **Clear Problem Formulation and Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the problem setup, the UTRL framework, and the design choices behind the reward functions.
    *   **Comparative Study to CURE:** The rigorous comparison with the concurrent study CURE and the effort to make the implementation setup equivalent shows the care and attention to fair evaluation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Dependency:** While UTRL reduces the need for unit test *labels*, it still relies on a dataset of programming instructions and *code solutions* (TACO). The framework's effectiveness might be limited if adapted to a domain where even solution examples are scarce. The paper could provide more clarity regarding UTRL's robustness to variations in the training dataset's quality or diversity.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** RL algorithms are often sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of how the hyperparameters were selected and the potential impact of different hyperparameter settings on UTRL's performance.
    *   **Limited Ablation on Reward Components:** While there is an ablation study on the validity reward, more insight would be gained if ablations were conducted on the discrimination reward and the interaction between it and the validity reward.
    *   **Code Generator Training:** The method for training the code generator only considers whether code passes all tests in a test suite, which doesn't offer a granular measure of correctness and may hurt the efficiency of the code generator training.

*   **Potential Influence:** UTRL has the potential to influence future research on automated unit test generation, code generation, and reinforcement learning for programming tasks. It presents a compelling alternative to supervised fine-tuning and introduces a novel approach to defining reward functions based on adversarial interaction.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and effective approach to training LLMs for unit test generation using an adversarial RL framework. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating the superiority of UTRL over SFT and even outperforming frontier models like GPT-4.1. By eliminating the need for explicit unit test labels, UTRL offers a more scalable solution. However, the limitations regarding dataset dependency, hyperparameter sensitivity, code generator training, and limited ablation study prevent a higher score. Despite these limitations, UTRL represents a significant contribution to the field and has the potential to influence future research directions.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### R-4B: Incentivizing General-Purpose Auto-Thinking Capability in MLLMs via Bi-Mode Annealing and Reinforce Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21113v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Jiang, Qi Yang, Bolin Ni, Shiming Xiang, Han Hu, Houwen Peng
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) equipped with step-by-step thinking capabilities have demonstrated remarkable performance on complex reasoning problems. However, this thinking process is redundant for simple problems solvable without complex reasoning. To address this inefficiency, we propose R-4B, an auto-thinking MLLM, which can adaptively decide when to think based on problem complexity. The central idea of R-4B is to empower the model with both thinking and non-thinking capabilities using bi-mode annealing, and apply Bi-mode Policy Optimization~(BPO) to improve the model's accuracy in determining whether to activate the thinking process. Specifically, we first train the model on a carefully curated dataset spanning various topics, which contains samples from both thinking and non-thinking modes. Then it undergoes a second phase of training under an improved GRPO framework, where the policy model is forced to generate responses from both modes for each input query. Experimental results show that R-4B achieves state-of-the-art performance across 25 challenging benchmarks. It outperforms Qwen2.5-VL-7B in most tasks and achieves performance comparable to larger models such as Kimi-VL-A3B-Thinking-2506 (16B) on reasoning-intensive benchmarks with lower computational cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R-4B, a multimodal large language model (MLLM) designed with the capacity for "auto-thinking." R-4B adaptively determines when to engage in detailed, step-by-step reasoning based on the complexity of a given problem, rather than always using it. The core innovation lies in training R-4B using "bi-mode annealing," equipping the model with both thinking and non-thinking capabilities, and a "Bi-mode Policy Optimization (BPO)" reinforcement learning framework to refine the model's accuracy in deciding when to activate the thinking process. The model is first trained on a curated dataset containing samples requiring either thinking or direct responses.  Subsequently, it undergoes a second training phase under BPO, where the policy model is compelled to generate responses from both modes for each input. Experimental results on 25 benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, outperforming comparable models and, in some reasoning-intensive tasks, even matching larger models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of auto-thinking in MLLMs is not entirely new, but this paper makes several significant contributions. Existing approaches often rely on manual complexity analysis during data preparation or complex reward functions in reinforcement learning. R-4B's bi-mode annealing and BPO approach appear to be novel in their simplicity and effectiveness. The bi-mode annealing strategy, combining curated thinking and non-thinking data, offers a refined training methodology. The use of a rule-based mathematical reward within BPO contributes to the elimination of intricate reward function dependency, a weakness observed in similar studies. The approach allows the model to efficiently discern when to employ complex processes and when to revert to quicker, simpler responses.

*   **Significance:** The primary significance of the work lies in improving the efficiency of MLLMs. Always-thinking behavior, while boosting accuracy in complex problems, introduces redundancy and increases computation cost when applied to simpler queries. R-4B's ability to adaptively engage in thinking offers a promising pathway toward developing more efficient and resource-conscious MLLMs. Demonstrating state-of-the-art results across a diverse range of benchmarks further strengthens its impact. Additionally, the paper offers R-4B's framework to stimulate further research within MLLMs. The paper is also significant in showing how an auto-thinking model performs better compared to the general state-of-the-art.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The bi-mode annealing technique and the BPO algorithm are well-motivated and elegantly designed.
    *   The experimental results are compelling, showcasing superior performance against strong baselines across many benchmarks.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the technical details of the proposed method.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While BPO appears simpler than other RL approaches, the paper could provide more details on its sensitivity to hyperparameters or its computational overhead. The simplicity that stems from its rule-based design may, in fact, be difficult to adapt or scale within different environments.
    *   Although the model is tested across many benchmarks, further analysis of specific failure cases would be valuable. Understanding when and why R-4B incorrectly activates or deactivates thinking would better highlight its limitations and provide direction for future improvement.
    *   The paper does rely on the Qwen2.5-32B-VL model for annotation which means that any errors or biases within this model would be inherited.
    * The "thinking atrophy" explanation of performance degradation is qualitative; quantitative evidence or more thorough analyses may augment understanding.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the R-4B approach proves to be robust and scalable, it could have a significant influence on the development of future MLLMs. Its emphasis on efficiency could make MLLMs more accessible and practical for real-world applications.  Furthermore, its approach of leveraging both reasoning and direct-answer capabilities may encourage more research into adaptive and context-aware AI systems. Its release as an open-source design would permit more efficient iteration, experimentation, and refinement.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** R-4B presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of MLLMs, offering a promising pathway toward more efficient and adaptable models. The bi-mode annealing and BPO approach are elegantly designed and backed by compelling experimental results. The paper's weaknesses, such as the limited information on hyperparameter tuning and the lack of failure case analysis, slightly temper its score. Nevertheless, its emphasis on efficient resource utilization and its potential influence on future MLLM design justify a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### How Does Cognitive Bias Affect Large Language Models? A Case Study on the Anchoring Effect in Price Negotiation Simulations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21137v1)
- **Authors**: Yoshiki Takenami, Yin Jou Huang, Yugo Murawaki, Chenhui Chu
- **Abstract**: Cognitive biases, well-studied in humans, can also be observed in LLMs, affecting their reliability in real-world applications. This paper investigates the anchoring effect in LLM-driven price negotiations. To this end, we instructed seller LLM agents to apply the anchoring effect and evaluated negotiations using not only an objective metric but also a subjective metric. Experimental results show that LLMs are influenced by the anchoring effect like humans. Additionally, we investigated the relationship between the anchoring effect and factors such as reasoning and personality. It was shown that reasoning models are less prone to the anchoring effect, suggesting that the long chain of thought mitigates the effect. However, we found no significant correlation between personality traits and susceptibility to the anchoring effect. These findings contribute to a deeper understanding of cognitive biases in LLMs and to the realization of safe and responsible application of LLMs in society.
- **Summary**: This paper investigates the anchoring effect in LLM-driven price negotiations, instructing seller LLM agents to apply the anchoring effect and evaluating negotiations using objective and subjective metrics. It explores the relationship between the anchoring effect and factors like reasoning and personality. The experiments show that LLMs are influenced by the anchoring effect, and reasoning models are less prone to it, suggesting mitigation through long chains of thought. However, no significant correlation was found between personality traits and susceptibility to the anchoring effect.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper builds upon existing work simulating human behavior with LLMs, particularly in price negotiation. The key novelties are:
1.  **Flexibility in Anchor Setting:** The paper gives LLMs more freedom in setting the anchor compared to previous work that used pre-defined coefficients, which makes the paper more generalizable.
2.  **Subjective Metric Incorporation:** It includes a subjective metric (satisfaction) alongside the objective utility to evaluate negotiation outcomes. This aligns with findings from behavioral economics about the importance of subjective perceptions.
3.  **Reasoning Model Investigation:** It specifically explores the impact of a reasoning model (OpenAI's o1) on the anchoring effect.
4.  **Personality Trait analysis:** it explictly analyzes correlation between personality traits and anchoring effects in LLMs.

While LLM-based simulations of human behavior are becoming more common, these specific additions contribute to the understanding of LLM behavior in a negotiation context.

**Significance:** The findings have implications for the safe and responsible application of LLMs in real-world settings, particularly in scenarios involving decision-making and negotiation. The demonstration that reasoning models can mitigate the anchoring effect is a useful insight. The negative result concerning personality traits, though contrasting with some human studies, is still a valuable contribution to the understanding of differences between human and LLM cognition. These results also enhance understanding of how LLMs behave against cognitive biases.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined research question:** The paper clearly articulates the research questions and objectives.
*   **Rigorous methodology:** The experimental setup is well-described, with clear explanations of the conditions, prompts, and evaluation metrics.
*   **Interesting findings:** The results about reasoning are thought-provoking and warrant further investigation.
*   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses solely on the anchoring effect and price negotiation, which restricts the generalizability of the findings to other cognitive biases or decision-making contexts.
*   **Model Selection:** While the use of various LLMs is a strength, a more detailed comparison and discussion of the architectural differences and training data of these models might provide further insights into the observed behaviors.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While the paper presents some related work on humans, the connections could have been made more strongly. The paper does not thoroughly theoretically justify why certain aspects or results should be the same between humans and LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite the limitations, the paper makes a meaningful contribution by exploring a specific cognitive bias in LLMs and investigating the impact of reasoning. The findings, while not groundbreaking, are relevant to the broader goal of understanding and controlling LLM behavior. The combination of objective and subjective metrics is a welcome addition to the methodology in this area. As such, it deserves a score indicating moderate novelty and significance.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Adaptive LLM Routing under Budget Constraints
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21141v1)
- **Authors**: Pranoy Panda, Raghav Magazine, Chaitanya Devaguptapu, Sho Takemori, Vishal Sharma
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have revolutionized natural language processing, but their varying capabilities and costs pose challenges in practical applications. LLM routing addresses this by dynamically selecting the most suitable LLM for each query/task. Previous approaches treat this as a supervised learning problem, assuming complete knowledge of optimal query-LLM pairings. However, real-world scenarios lack such comprehensive mappings and face evolving user queries. We thus propose to study LLM routing as a contextual bandit problem, enabling adaptive decision-making using bandit feedback without requiring exhaustive inference across all LLMs for all queries (in contrast to supervised routing). To address this problem, we develop a shared embedding space for queries and LLMs, where query and LLM embeddings are aligned to reflect their affinity. This space is initially learned from offline human preference data and refined through online bandit feedback. We instantiate this idea through Preference-prior Informed Linucb fOr adaptive rouTing (PILOT), a novel extension of LinUCB. To handle diverse user budgets for model routing, we introduce an online cost policy modeled as a multi-choice knapsack problem, ensuring resource-efficient routing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of efficiently routing queries to the most appropriate Large Language Model (LLM) under budget constraints.  Instead of relying on supervised learning with complete query-LLM pairings (which are expensive to obtain), it frames LLM routing as a contextual bandit problem. The key contributions include: (1) A shared embedding space for queries and LLMs, initially learned from human preference data and refined with online bandit feedback.  This allows representing query-LLM affinity as a distance. (2) PILOT (Preference-Prior Informed LinUCB), an extension of LinUCB that leverages offline human preference data to initialize the learning process. (3) An online cost policy, modeled as a multi-choice knapsack problem, to dynamically allocate resources within budget constraints.  Experiments demonstrate that PILOT outperforms existing bandit baselines in terms of regret and performance across various datasets and cost budgets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several elements: (a) Framing LLM routing as a *contextual bandit problem under budget constraints*. This is a valuable perspective shift. (b)  Leveraging readily available *human preference data* for pre-training the shared embedding space to seed the online bandit learning process.  (c) The PILOT algorithm itself, which adapts LinUCB with a preference-informed prior, although the novelty of this adaptation is incremental. (d) The online cost policy modeled as a multi-choice knapsack problem is also novel in the context of LLM routing.  While individual components exist, the *integrated approach* offers a practical contribution.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a *highly relevant and practical problem* in the deployment of LLMs.  The high cost and varying capabilities of different LLMs necessitate intelligent routing. By reducing reliance on expensive labeled datasets and enabling online adaptation, the paper offers a *more scalable and adaptable* solution.  The empirical results support the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The results demonstrating that Pilot is on par with or surpasses HybridLLM a *supervised* router, even though Pilot is trained solely on *bandit* feedback, is compelling. The analysis of how Pilot adapts to shifted query distributions is also important. The authors also justify their method theoretically using a regret bound analysis.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Problem Formulation:** The formulation of LLM routing as a contextual bandit problem is well-motivated and more practical than purely supervised approaches.
    * **Integration of Offline and Online Learning:**  Effectively combines offline human preference data with online bandit feedback, leading to faster and more robust learning.
    * **Budget Constraint Handling:** The online cost policy provides a mechanism for practical deployment, allowing users to control costs while maximizing performance.
    * **Empirical Validation:**  Comprehensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach on a diverse dataset and against strong baselines.
    * **Theoretical Justification:** Regret bound analysis lends theoretical credibility.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Algorithmic Novelty:**  PILOT is essentially LinUCB with a specific initialization strategy. While effective, the algorithmic contribution is less groundbreaking than the overall problem framing and system design.
    * **Limited Generalizability of Preference Data:** The effectiveness of the pre-training phase relies on the availability and quality of human preference data. The transferability of preference data across different application domains could be a concern.
    * **Cost Policy Assumptions:** The cost policy assumes relatively small query costs relative to budget. Real-world scenarios with queries that are substantial fractions of budget would need further consideration.
   * **Limited Deployment Setting Evaluation:** More real-world deployment setting should be conducted for more general evaluation.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    * **Shifting Research Focus:** Encouraging more research on bandit-based LLM routing techniques.
    * **Providing a Practical Solution:**  Offering a viable solution for efficient LLM deployment in cost-sensitive applications.
    * **Informing Algorithm Design:**  Highlighting the benefits of leveraging human preference data for initializing online learning algorithms.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid, significant contribution to the field of LLM routing. While the algorithmic novelty of PILOT itself is incremental, the framing of the problem, the integration of human preference data, the online cost policy, and the comprehensive empirical validation create a practical and impactful solution. The limitations around preference data availability and assumptions of the cost policy are noteworthy, but do not fundamentally detract from the value of the work.
